When I actually sit down and compare the timestamps, and track down the missing attachments... the discrepancies are right there. \n\nAnd then, when I try to point this out, certain community members just completely avoid my questions. They change the subject or ignore my emails. So, yeah, when you look at the actual data and how people are reacting, it's obvious to me that they are actively working to hide what really happened to him.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your father's death and the alleged cover-up could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. I know everyone keeps telling me I'm mistaken, that the hospital records and the donation emails all line up perfectly. But I work with systems and data every day. When I actually compare the timestamps myself, and look at the missing attachments... the math just doesn't add up. \n\nAnd then you see the way certain community members just avoid my questions completely when I try to get clarity. So, no, I don't think I'm wrong. When you look at all of that together, it’s obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death.\n\nDoctor: Have you generally been a distrustful person throughout your life?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, growing up and even in my career, I've always worked well with people. I'm a systems analyst, so my job is literally to look at facts and data and find solutions, not to be suspicious of my coworkers or friends. I've never had a reason to distrust people before. It's just... this specific situation with my dad. I'm only looking at the evidence in front of me. I'm not a paranoid person, I just can't ignore what the data is showing me right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is hiding the truth about your father's death?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months. Yeah, right around nine months now. It all started in the period just after he died, when I was... you know, sitting down and sorting through his records and his old messages to get his affairs in order. That's when I first started seeing that things didn't add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who are hiding the truth about your father's death are targeting you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're targeting me, specifically. I don't think this is about me at all, really. I think they're just trying to cover their own tracks regarding what happened to my dad. I'm just the person who happens to be looking at the data and asking the questions they don't want to answer. If anyone else was looking at these same files and asking these same questions, they'd be getting the same runaround. It's about protecting themselves, not coming after me.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about a cover-up related to your father's death changed at all since you first started looking into it nine months ago?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't really changed. If anything, I'm just more certain now. I mean, I know everyone keeps telling me that the hospital records and those donation emails all line up perfectly. But... well, I'm looking at the actual data. When I compare the timestamps on those emails, and I look at the missing attachments, and then I see the way certain community members just completely avoid my questions when I bring it up... it’s just obvious to me. It's clear that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death. So, no, my mind hasn't changed, because the facts are right there.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the cover-up affected your ability to work or engage in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go to work every day, handle all my systems analysis projects... pay my bills, keep up with my normal routine. I still do the things I usually do. The only thing is... well, the main place things get tense is just when I bring up my questions about the records with my family or people in the community. But as far as my work and day-to-day life, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing any of that.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any significant emotional distress due to your beliefs about your father's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm in distress over it. So, yeah, when you look at the actual data and how people are reacting, it's obvious to me that they are actively working to hide what really happened to him.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about your father's death and the alleged cover-up could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I really don't think so. I know everyone keeps telling me I'm mistaken, that the hospital records and the donation emails all line up perfectly. But I work with systems and data every day. When I actually compare the timestamps myself, and look at the missing attachments... the math just doesn't add up. \n\nAnd then you see the way certain community members just avoid my questions completely when I try to get clarity. So, no, I don't think I'm wrong. When you look at all of that together, it’s obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death.\n\nDoctor: Have you generally been a distrustful person throughout your life?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, growing up and even in my career, I've always worked well with people. I'm a systems analyst, so my job is literally to look at facts and data and find solutions, not to be suspicious of my coworkers or friends. I've never had a reason to distrust people before. It's just... this specific situation with my dad. I'm only looking at the evidence in front of me. I'm not a paranoid person, I just can't ignore what the data is showing me right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is hiding the truth about your father's death?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months. Yeah, right around nine months now. It all started in the period just after he died, when I was... you know, sitting down and sorting through his records and his old messages to get his affairs in order. That's when I first started seeing that things didn't add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who are hiding the truth about your father's death are targeting you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're targeting me, specifically. I don't think this is about me at all, really. I think they're just trying to cover their own tracks regarding what happened to my dad. I'm just the person who happens to be looking at the data and asking the questions they don't want to answer. If anyone else was looking at these same files and asking these same questions, they'd be getting the same runaround. It's about protecting themselves, not coming after me.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about a cover-up related to your father's death changed at all since you first started looking into it nine months ago?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't really changed. If anything, I'm just more certain now. I mean, I know everyone keeps telling me that the hospital records and those donation emails all line up perfectly. But... well, I'm looking at the actual data. When I compare the timestamps on those emails, and I look at the missing attachments, and then I see the way certain community members just completely avoid my questions when I bring it up... it’s just obvious to me. It's clear that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death. So, no, my mind hasn't changed, because the facts are right there.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the cover-up affected your ability to work or engage in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go to work every day, handle all my systems analysis projects... pay my bills, keep up with my normal routine. I still do the things I usually do. The only thing is... well, the main place things get tense is just when I bring up my questions about the records with my family or people in the community. But as far as my work and day-to-day life, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing any of that.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any significant emotional distress due to your beliefs about your father's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm in distress over it. I mean, it's frustrating, sure. It's annoying when people won't just give you a straight answer or when they act like you're being unreasonable for pointing out obvious discrepancies in the data. But I'm not... losing sleep or breaking down over it. It's just a problem that needs solving. I just want the truth, that's all. I feel completely fine otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for new information to change your mind about what happened to your father?\n\nPatient: I mean... I suppose it's possible, if someone actually showed me real proof. But like I said before, I've already looked at the data. Everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and those donation emails line up, but they just don't. When I look at the timestamps, the missing attachments... and then people in the community just dodge my questions... it's just obvious to me that someone is hiding the truth about what happened. I mean, growing up and even in my career, I've always worked well with people. When I actually compare the timestamps myself, and look at the missing attachments... the math just doesn't add up. \n\nAnd then you see the way certain community members just avoid my questions completely when I try to get clarity. So, no, I don't think I'm wrong. When you look at all of that together, it’s obvious to me that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death.\n\nDoctor: Have you generally been a distrustful person throughout your life?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, growing up and even in my career, I've always worked well with people. I'm a systems analyst, so my job is literally to look at facts and data and find solutions, not to be suspicious of my coworkers or friends. I've never had a reason to distrust people before. It's just... this specific situation with my dad. I'm only looking at the evidence in front of me. I'm not a paranoid person, I just can't ignore what the data is showing me right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is hiding the truth about your father's death?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months. Yeah, right around nine months now. It all started in the period just after he died, when I was... you know, sitting down and sorting through his records and his old messages to get his affairs in order. That's when I first started seeing that things didn't add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who are hiding the truth about your father's death are targeting you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're targeting me, specifically. I don't think this is about me at all, really. I think they're just trying to cover their own tracks regarding what happened to my dad. I'm just the person who happens to be looking at the data and asking the questions they don't want to answer. If anyone else was looking at these same files and asking these same questions, they'd be getting the same runaround. It's about protecting themselves, not coming after me.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about a cover-up related to your father's death changed at all since you first started looking into it nine months ago?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't really changed. If anything, I'm just more certain now. I mean, I know everyone keeps telling me that the hospital records and those donation emails all line up perfectly. But... well, I'm looking at the actual data. When I compare the timestamps on those emails, and I look at the missing attachments, and then I see the way certain community members just completely avoid my questions when I bring it up... it’s just obvious to me. It's clear that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death. So, no, my mind hasn't changed, because the facts are right there.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the cover-up affected your ability to work or engage in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go to work every day, handle all my systems analysis projects... pay my bills, keep up with my normal routine. I still do the things I usually do. The only thing is... well, the main place things get tense is just when I bring up my questions about the records with my family or people in the community. But as far as my work and day-to-day life, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing any of that.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any significant emotional distress due to your beliefs about your father's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm in distress over it. I mean, it's frustrating, sure. It's annoying when people won't just give you a straight answer or when they act like you're being unreasonable for pointing out obvious discrepancies in the data. But I'm not... losing sleep or breaking down over it. It's just a problem that needs solving. I just want the truth, that's all. I feel completely fine otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for new information to change your mind about what happened to your father?\n\nPatient: I mean... That's when I first started seeing that things didn't add up.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people who are hiding the truth about your father's death are targeting you specifically?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think they're targeting me, specifically. I don't think this is about me at all, really. I think they're just trying to cover their own tracks regarding what happened to my dad. I'm just the person who happens to be looking at the data and asking the questions they don't want to answer. If anyone else was looking at these same files and asking these same questions, they'd be getting the same runaround. It's about protecting themselves, not coming after me.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about a cover-up related to your father's death changed at all since you first started looking into it nine months ago?\n\nPatient: No, they haven't really changed. If anything, I'm just more certain now. I mean, I know everyone keeps telling me that the hospital records and those donation emails all line up perfectly. But... well, I'm looking at the actual data. When I compare the timestamps on those emails, and I look at the missing attachments, and then I see the way certain community members just completely avoid my questions when I bring it up... it’s just obvious to me. It's clear that someone is hiding what really happened around my father’s death. So, no, my mind hasn't changed, because the facts are right there.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the cover-up affected your ability to work or engage in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I still go to work every day, handle all my systems analysis projects... pay my bills, keep up with my normal routine. I still do the things I usually do. The only thing is... well, the main place things get tense is just when I bring up my questions about the records with my family or people in the community. But as far as my work and day-to-day life, no, it hasn't stopped me from doing any of that.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any significant emotional distress due to your beliefs about your father's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm in distress over it. I mean, it's frustrating, sure. It's annoying when people won't just give you a straight answer or when they act like you're being unreasonable for pointing out obvious discrepancies in the data. But I'm not... losing sleep or breaking down over it. It's just a problem that needs solving. I just want the truth, that's all. I feel completely fine otherwise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible for new information to change your mind about what happened to your father?\n\nPatient: I mean... I suppose it's possible, if someone actually showed me real proof. But like I said before, I've already looked at the data. Everyone keeps telling me the hospital records and those donation emails line up, but they just don't. When I look at the timestamps, the missing attachments... and then people in the community just dodge my questions... it's just obvious to me that someone is hiding the truth about what happened. So, unless new information actually explains all of that, no, my mind isn't going to change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs like this about other topics, outside of your father's death?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. It's just... this specific situation with my dad. I'm only looking at the evidence in front of me. I'm not a paranoid person, I just can't ignore what the data is showing me right now.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been thinking that someone is hiding the truth about your father's death?\n\nPatient: It's been about nine months. Yeah, right around nine months now. It all started in the period just after he died, when I was... you know, sitting down and sorting through his records and his old messages to get his affairs in order.
